<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct nonzero perverse Weil Hecke eigensheaves for SL<sub><i>n</i></sub> with Borel level at one marked point, after a finite extension of the field of constants. The parameter is a geometrically dense arithmetic PGL<sub><i>n</i></sub>-local system with tame regular-unipotent monodromy on a once-punctured curve of genus at least two over a finite field of characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; <i>n</i>. The full multi-leg eigenstructure is Frobenius compatible with the prescribed arithmetic eigenvalue, and the geometric sheaf has parabolic nilpotent singular support.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\geq2$, let $\mathbb F_q$ have characteristic $p>n$, let $\ell\ne p$, and put $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$. Let $X_0/\mathbb F_q$ be a smooth projective geometrically connected curve of genus at least two, with $x_0\in X_0(\mathbb F_q)$, and put $U_0=X_0\setminus\{x_0\}$. Suppose $$\rho:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U_0)\longrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_n(L)$$ is continuous for a finite extension $L/\mathbb Q_\ell$, its geometric restriction is Zariski dense, and its inertia at $x_0$ kills wild inertia and has the form $$\label{eq:regular-monodromy} \rho(\gamma)=\exp\bigl(t_\ell(\gamma)N\bigr)$$ for a regular nilpotent $N$. Here a choice of compatible $\ell$-power roots of unity identifies the tame quotient $\mathbb Z_\ell(1)$ with $\mathbb Z_\ell$ and defines $t_\ell$.* *There are an integer $m\geq1$ and a nonzero $E$-adic Weil sheaf $M_0$ on $$\operatorname{Bun}_{\mathrm{SL}_n,B,x_0}(X_0)_{\mathbb F_{q^m}}$$ whose geometric pullback $M$ is locally constructible perverse and has singular support in $\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}$. If $\rho_m$ denotes the restriction of $\rho$ to $\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U_{0,\mathbb F_{q^m}})$, then for every finite set $I$ and every family $V_i\in\operatorname{Rep}_E(\mathrm{PGL}_n)$ there are isomorphisms of Weil sheaves $$\label{eq:main-eigenmaps} \mathsf H_{I,\boldsymbol V}(M_0) \simeq M_0\boxtimes\mathop{\boxtimes}_{i\in I}(V_i)_{\rho_m}.$$ They are natural in the representations and coherent for the unit, convolution, permutations, and all fusion diagonals.
  - Explanation: Constructs a nonzero perverse Weil eigensheaf at ordinary Borel level after a finite extension of constants, preserving the prescribed arithmetic eigenvalue and the entire fusion-compatible eigenstructure.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-satake-transport)): Semilinear isomorphisms of pointed curves identify the relative Satake and Hecke systems, compatibly with all the operations in Definition <a href="#def:eigenstructure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:eigenstructure">2</a>. In representation labels the required symmetric tensor comparison is unique. The same assertion holds for extension between algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields.
  - Explanation: Transport identifies the full relative Hecke system with a unique symmetric tensor comparison on representation labels.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-weil-satake)): Over a finite field, the split Weil structures on <a href="#eq:satake-normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:satake-normalization">[eq:satake-normalization]</a> give a symmetric tensor Satake system, with ordinary coefficient multiplicity spaces carrying trivial action. Its Frobenius comparison is the one in Lemma <a href="#lem:satake-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:satake-transport">3</a>.
  - Explanation: Split normalized Weil structures make the tensor comparisons Frobenius compatible and leave coefficient multiplicity spaces with trivial action.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-equivariant-specialization)): In this setup, geometric nearby cycles preserve local bounded constructibility and are perverse exact. They admit natural isomorphisms $$\label{eq:hecke-specialization} \mathsf H^s_{I,\boldsymbol V}(\Psi F) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \Psi\bigl(\mathsf H^{\bar\eta}_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F)\bigr)$$ compatible with the unit, convolution, permutations, and every fusion diagonal. Suppose $F$ has a coherent eigenstructure with parameter $\vartheta_{\bar\eta}$. For each representation, suppose its evaluation sheaf has a lisse lattice whose finite reductions extend lisse after finite trait extensions, compatibly on further extensions and with the reductions of a special-fiber tensor system $\vartheta_s$. Then $\Psi F$ has a coherent eigenstructure with eigenvalue $\vartheta_s$.* *If the models, $F$ with its given coherent eigenstructure, and these tensor-specialization data carry a semilinear generator, with a compatible lift to $\bar\eta$, all the comparisons and the resulting eigenstructure are equivariant. For the finite-stage lattice models one may use compatible invariant trait extensions. No single finite extension is required for all reductions.
  - Explanation: Geometric nearby cycles preserve perversity and carry a coherent eigenstructure through compatible finite-reduction specialization data, equivariantly when those data carry a semilinear action.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-lie-hypotheses)): For $G=\mathrm{SL}_n$ over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero or of characteristic $p>n$, the hypotheses H1–H4 of [[CT](#ref-CT), Theorem 1.1] hold.
  - Explanation: The stated characteristic regime supplies the Lie-theoretic hypotheses needed by the geometric input.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-equivariant-unramified)): Let $C$ be a smooth projective connected curve of genus at least two over an algebraically closed field $K$ of characteristic zero. Let $f$ be a semilinear automorphism of $C$ with an ample line bundle $\mathcal L$ and an isomorphism $f^*\mathcal L\simeq\mathcal L$. Let $G=\mathrm{SL}_n$ and $\check G=\mathrm{PGL}_n$. Suppose that $\tau$ is a continuous $\check G$-local system on $C$, defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$, with Zariski-dense image and an isomorphism $u:f^*\tau\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau$.* *There is a nonzero locally bounded constructible complex $D_\tau$ on $\operatorname{Bun}_G(C)$, with nilpotent singular support, a full coherent Hecke eigenstructure of eigenvalue $\tau$, and an isomorphism $$b:f^*D_\tau\xrightarrow{\sim}D_\tau$$ compatible with that eigenstructure and $u$. The object $D_\tau$ is compact in the automorphic nilpotent category. The eigenstructure uses the relative Satake normalization of Section <a href="#sec:conventions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conventions">2</a> and is compatible with unit, convolution, permutations, and every fusion diagonal. The isomorphisms $b$ and $u$ define compatible actions of $\mathbb Z$ on the eigencomplex and its eigenvalue.
  - Explanation: An invariant dense unramified parameter on a polarized characteristic-zero curve yields a compact equivariant eigencomplex with nilpotent singular support.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-spectral-action-transport)): Semilinear transport by $f$ identifies the automorphic spectral action with the action for the transported curve and its restricted stack of local systems. Consequently, if $f^*\tau\simeq\tau$, transport preserves the summand of $\mathcal D_C$ corresponding to $\mathcal S_\tau$.
  - Explanation: Transport of the entire spectral action preserves the summand associated with an invariant parameter.
- **C8** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-point-object)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)$ be a commutative local $\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$-algebra with residue field $\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$, and let $P$ be a nonzero bounded complex of $R$-modules whose cohomology modules have finite length. Suppose that $$\operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(P,P)=\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell},\qquad \operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(P,P[j])=0\quad(j<0).$$ Then $P\simeq\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}[r]$ for an integer $r$.
  - Explanation: Finite-length cohomology, scalar endomorphisms and vanishing negative self-extensions characterize a residue-field object up to shift.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-unramified-eigenstructure-unique)): The complex $D_\tau$ admits a unique full coherent Hecke eigenstructure with eigenvalue $\tau$. Consequently every isomorphism $f^*D_\tau\simeq D_\tau$ is compatible with the eigenstructure and the given isomorphism $u:f^*\tau\simeq\tau$.
  - Explanation: The dense parameter has trivial centralizer, forcing uniqueness of the full coherent eigenstructure and compatibility of every transport isomorphism.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-lifted-parameter)): The arithmetic representation $\rho$ determines a continuous parameter $$\sigma_1:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}((U_1)_{\overline D}) \longrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_n(L')$$ for a finite coefficient extension $L'/L$, together with equivariance under $\varphi$, with the following properties.* 1) *Its geometric image is Zariski dense. After compatible choices of frames it is the same compact subgroup as $\rho(\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U_{0,k}))$.* 2) *Its boundary homomorphism is $$\label{eq:lifted-boundary} \gamma\longmapsto\exp(t_\ell(\gamma)N_1),$$ where $N_1$ is regular nilpotent. Every prime factor of characteristic-zero inertia other than $\ell$ acts trivially.* 3) *In compatible frames at the coordinate-neutralized root gerbes of $x_1$, the equivariance has a single value $A\in H_0$, where $H_0=\rho(\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U_0))$, and $$\label{eq:frobenius-scales-nilpotent} \operatorname{Ad}(A)N_1=qN_1.$$* 4) *For every algebraic representation of $\mathrm{PGL}_n$, the associated local system over $(U_1)_K$ admits an invariant lattice whose finite reductions extend lisse over $\mathcal U_R$, equivariantly under $\varphi$. Their specializations recover the tensor local system of $\rho$, including its given arithmetic action.
  - Explanation: Lifts the arithmetic parameter through finite torsor towers, retaining the compact geometric image, boundary homomorphism and semilinear action.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-component-orbits)): Let $S$ be the spectrum of a complete strictly henselian discrete valuation ring with algebraically closed residue field, and let $\mathcal Y\to S$ be a proper flat tame Deligne–Mumford curve with finite diagonal, projective coarse space, and geometrically reduced fibers. Let $\mathcal T\to\mathcal Y$ be a finite étale right $Q$-torsor, for a finite group $Q$, and let $y:S\to\mathcal Y$ be a section in its smooth scheme locus. Identify the geometric special and generic fibers of $y^*\mathcal T$ by a trivialization over $S$. The partitions of this finite set by connected components of $\mathcal T_{\bar s}$ and $\mathcal T_{\bar\eta}$ then agree.
  - Explanation: Component partitions of a finite torsor agree across the proper tame family when compared in a trivialized section fiber.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-conjugator)): Suppose $N_1\in\mathfrak{pgl}_n(L')$ is regular nilpotent, $A\in\mathrm{PGL}_n(L')$ satisfies <a href="#eq:frobenius-scales-nilpotent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:frobenius-scales-nilpotent">[eq:frobenius-scales-nilpotent]</a>, and $H_0\subset\mathrm{PGL}_n(L')$ is compact with $A\in H_0$. After a finite coefficient extension there exist a cocharacter $c:\mathbb G_m\to\mathrm{PGL}_n$, a compact open subgroup $J$ containing $H_0$, and an integer $e>1$ prime to $\ell$, such that $$\label{eq:compact-conjugator} c(z)A=Ac(z),\qquad \operatorname{Ad}(c(z))N_1=zN_1, \qquad P:=c(-1/e)\in J.$$ Consequently $P$ commutes with $A$ and $$\label{eq:inverse-boundary-conjugation} P\exp(-e aN_1)P^{-1}=\exp(aN_1) \qquad(a\in L').$$
  - Explanation: Constructs a compact conjugator commuting with the arithmetic action and correcting the reversed, multiplied boundary monodromy; the cover degree is prime to the coefficient prime.
- **C13** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-nodal-parameter)): There exists a continuous Zariski-dense parameter $$\tau:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(C)\longrightarrow J \subset\mathrm{PGL}_n(L')$$ with equivariance under $\varphi$. For every algebraic representation of $\mathrm{PGL}_n$, an invariant lattice in its evaluation local system has finite reductions extending lisse over $\mathcal V\times_{S_{b_*}}S_{b_r}$ for sufficiently large $r$. These extensions are equivariant and specialize on the two punctured components to $\sigma_1$ and $\sigma_2$, with their given actions, compatibly with the entire tensor system.
  - Explanation: Gluing compatible finite torsors on a balanced degeneration supplies a dense compact-valued generic parameter with equivariant tensor specialization data.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-charzero-equivariant)): Let $(C_1,x_1)/D$, $\sigma_1$, and $\varphi$ be the pointed curve, dense lifted parameter, and semilinear automorphism of Proposition <a href="#prop:lifted-parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:lifted-parameter">11</a>. There exist an integer $m\geq 1$ and a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric $\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$-adic sheaf $$M_{\overline D}\quad\text{on}\quad \mathcal A_1=\operatorname{Bun}_{G,B,x_1}((C_1)_{\overline D})$$ with a $\varphi^m$-structure and the full coherent Hecke eigenstructure for $\sigma_1$. Every eigenisomorphism is compatible with this structure and with the given $\varphi^m$-structure on $\sigma_1$. Moreover, $\operatorname{SS}(M_{\overline D})\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}$.
  - Explanation: Specialization at a prescribed node type, restriction to a component and enhancement descent produce a nonzero equivariant perverse eigensheaf with parabolic nilpotent singular support.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-specialization-detection)): Let $S=\operatorname{Spec}A$ be an excellent complete strictly henselian trait with algebraically closed residue field and $\ell$ invertible. Let $\mathcal Y\to S$ be smooth and locally of finite type. Its special fiber is to be an unlevelled, ordinary Borel-level, or enhanced Borel-level bundle stack on a smooth projective curve, or, in characteristic zero, the simultaneous-torus quotient of two enhanced-level stacks. If the residue characteristic is positive, assume that the residue field is an algebraic closure of a finite field and impose the Lie-theoretic hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:lie-hypotheses" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lie-hypotheses">6</a>.* *Assume that a smooth scheme $L\to S$ of relative dimension one provides spherical Hecke legs and that $L_s$ contains a nonempty open of each special-fiber curve on which modifications are allowed. The bounded output maps to $\mathcal Y\times_S L$ must be representable and proper; exact-relative-position strata must be smooth over both input-and-leg and output-and-leg; their special fibers must have the transverse-modification geometry of [[CT](#ref-CT), Proposition 3.3]. The Satake kernel on its open stratum is constant with the relative Satake shift and twist.* *Let $F$ be a locally bounded constructible complex on $\mathcal Y_{\bar\eta}$ with lisse spherical Hecke eigenvalues on $L_{\bar\eta}$. Suppose each eigenvalue has a lisse lattice whose finite reductions extend lisse after finite trait extensions, compatibly with the special-fiber value, as in Proposition <a href="#prop:equivariant-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:equivariant-specialization">5</a>. If, on some smooth finite-type chart $Z\to\mathcal Y$, the closure of the nonzero-stalk locus of $F\|_{Z_{\bar\eta}}$ meets $Z_s$, then $\Psi F\ne0$.
  - Explanation: A meeting of the generic nonzero-stalk closure with the special fiber detects nonzero geometric nearby cycles under the stated Hecke and lattice-extension hypotheses.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Coherent Hecke eigenstructure** ([source](#stmt-def-eigenstructure)): For a $\check G$-local system $\vartheta$ on $U$, a coherent Hecke eigenstructure on $F$ consists of isomorphisms $$\label{eq:eigenstructure} \mathsf H_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F) \simeq F\boxtimes\mathop{\boxtimes}_{i\in I}(V_i)_\vartheta$$ for all finite $I$, natural in the representations, and compatible with the unit, successive modifications and convolution, permutations, and all collision maps <a href="#eq:fusion-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fusion-convention">[eq:fusion-convention]</a>, including their iterated compatibilities.
- **Ordinary Borel-level bundle stack** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The stack $\operatorname{Bun}_{G,B,x}(X)$ parametrizes $G$-bundles on $X$ with a $B$-reduction at $x$.
- **Locally constructible perverse sheaf** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A sheaf on this stack is locally constructible perverse if its pullback to each smooth finite-type scheme chart, shifted by the relative dimension of that chart, is bounded constructible and perverse.
- **Parabolic global nilpotent cone** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its parabolic global nilpotent cone $\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}$ consists of generically nilpotent Higgs fields with at most a simple pole at $x$ and residue in the nilradical of the chosen Borel.
- **Weil structure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A Weil structure over $\mathbb F_{q^m}$ means an isomorphism between the $q^m$-Frobenius pullback of the geometric sheaf and the sheaf itself.
- **Enhanced flag** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An enhanced flag is a reduction to $R_u(B)$; forgetting the enhancement is a representable $T$-torsor $\mathcal A^+\to\mathcal A$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-lie-hypotheses) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lie-hypotheses): Trace pairing and block-Levi matrix arguments. -> Lie-theoretic specialization hypotheses.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-equivariant-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-equivariant-specialization): Naturality of nearby-cycle exchange maps and tensor transport. -> Equivariant full eigenstructure after nearby cycles.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-equivariant-unramified) / [proof](#sec:unramified): Spectral skyscraper recognition and rigidity on invariant finite-type opens. -> Compact equivariant unramified eigencomplex.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-lifted-parameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-component-orbits-2): Proper henselian lifting of compatible finite torsor towers. -> Dense lifted parameter retaining boundary and arithmetic action.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-conjugator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-conjugator): Diagonalize the arithmetic action along a regular Jordan chain and choose the cover degree by congruence. -> Compact conjugator commuting with the arithmetic action.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-nodal-parameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nodal-parameter): Balanced-node gluing and proper henselian lifting at every finite stage. -> Dense equivariant generic parameter.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-charzero-equivariant) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Nearby cycles on the prescribed node type and restriction to an invariant component point. -> Nonzero enhanced-level equivariant eigencomplex.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-charzero-equivariant) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Perverse truncation, unipotent enhancement monodromy and compact-support pushforward. -> Nonzero ordinary Borel-level perverse eigensheaf.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:final): Mixed-characteristic nearby cycles, support-closure detection and normalized Weil transport. -> Nonzero perverse Weil eigensheaf with parabolic nilpotent singular support.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **arithmetic_eigenstructure**: After a finite extension of the field of constants, a nonzero Weil eigensheaf has locally constructible perverse geometric pullback, parabolic nilpotent singular support, and a Frobenius-compatible full coherent eigenstructure.
- **equivariant_specialization**: Geometric nearby cycles transport a coherent eigenstructure and its semilinear action when the models and tensor specialization data carry compatible actions.
- **compact_gluing**: A compact conjugator commutes with the Frobenius value and identifies the oppositely oriented scaled boundary monodromy with the original boundary monodromy.
- **nonvanishing_detection**: Nearby cycles are nonzero when the closure of the generic nonzero-stalk locus meets the special fiber on a smooth finite-type chart, under the stated specialization hypotheses.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **forget_arithmetic**: Equip an arbitrary geometric eigensheaf with Frobenius on the underlying sheaf and regard the eigenstructure as automatically arithmetic. -> A Frobenius structure on the sheaf alone does not establish compatibility with the prescribed arithmetic eigenvalue.
- **arbitrary_gluing**: Glue inverse boundary monodromies using an arbitrary conjugation. -> The identification may fail to preserve the common Frobenius action or compact image.
- **automatic_nonvanishing**: Infer nonzero nearby cycles solely from a nonzero generic eigencomplex. -> The stated nonvanishing criterion has not been established.
- **profinite_descent**: Treat the resulting Weil structure as a proof of profinite descent. -> Compatibility with the cyclic Frobenius action does not supply the stronger descent assertion.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The asserted eigensheaf is obtained through a spectral equivalence, compatible finite torsor towers, geometric nearby cycles, and perverse cohomology. The tensor specialization permits different finite trait extensions for different reductions, and the Frobenius power is chosen by stabilizing an auxiliary point. The paper supplies no finite input representation or terminating procedure for producing the eigensheaf and its full coherent eigenstructure. Finite matrix identities in the gluing argument do not supply that missing construction.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-characteristic** (hypothesis): The main existence result assumes $n\geq2$, characteristic $p>n$, and $\ell\ne p$. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem outside the stated characteristic and coefficient regime.']
- **L-curve** (hypothesis): The curve is smooth, projective, geometrically connected, of genus at least two, with a rational marked point. Blocked use: ['Extending the result to arbitrary singular or nonprojective curves, or arbitrary marking data.']
- **L-density** (hypothesis): The parameter is continuous over a finite coefficient extension and its geometric restriction is Zariski dense. Blocked use: ['Using the uniqueness and transport argument for arbitrary nondense parameters.']
- **L-boundary** (hypothesis): Boundary inertia kills wild inertia and is the exponential of a regular nilpotent logarithm. Blocked use: ['Treating arbitrary wild or nonregular boundary monodromy as covered.']
- **L-constants** (edge_case): The asserted Weil sheaf may require a finite extension of the field of constants. Blocked use: ['Claiming existence over the original field of constants without further descent.']
- **L-coherence** (formal_scope): Frobenius compatibility is required for the entire eigenstructure. Blocked use: ['Substituting invariance of the underlying perverse sheaf for compatibility of the eigenisomorphisms.']
- **L-weil** (formal_scope): The conclusion uses a Weil structure; the proof does not assert profinite descent. Blocked use: ['Promoting the cyclic Frobenius action to a profinite descent assertion.']
- **L-finite-stages** (formal_scope): Finite lattice reductions may require compatible separate trait extensions. Blocked use: ['Demanding a single finite trait extension that accommodates the entire reduction tower.']
- **L-nearby-nonzero** (edge_case): A nonzero generic complex does not by itself guarantee nonzero nearby cycles. Blocked use: ['Skipping the support-closure meeting condition in the specialization argument.']
- **L-torus-nonzero** (edge_case): The enhancement-forgetting direct image uses a particular unipotent-monodromy nonvanishing argument. Blocked use: ['Assuming every torus direct image preserves nonvanishing.']
- **L-local-nilpotence** (formal_scope): The nilpotence bound used in the nonvanishing argument applies to the chosen finite-dimensional space. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform global nilpotence bound from this argument.']
- **L-conjugator-degree** (hypothesis): The auxiliary degree $e>1$ is prime to $\ell$; compactness and commutation with $A$ are part of the gluing conditions. Blocked use: ['Replacing the coefficient-prime condition with a condition prime to the curve characteristic.', 'Using inverse boundary monodromy alone to conclude compact equivariant gluing.']
- **L-unramified-perversity** (formal_scope): The unramified input supplies a complex; the perverse object is obtained later by truncation and removal of the enhancement. Blocked use: ['Claiming the unramified input already supplies the final Borel-level perverse sheaf.']
- **L-external-geometric-input** (formal_scope): The geometric companion results supply specialization and enhancement descent; arithmetic compatibility is established separately here. Blocked use: ['Treating the geometric input alone as Frobenius descent.']
- **L-effective-construction** (nonconstructive): The existence construction uses choices of equivalence and auxiliary geometric data; an effective procedure for producing the full Weil eigensheaf is not supplied. Blocked use: ['Interpreting finite algebraic checks as an executable construction or certificate for the full eigensheaf theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct nonzero perverse Weil Hecke eigensheaves for SL<sub><i>n</i></sub> with Borel level at one marked point, after a finite extension of the field of constants. The parameter is a geometrically dense arithmetic PGL<sub><i>n</i></sub>-local system with tame regular-unipotent monodromy on a once-punctured curve of genus at least two over a finite field of characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; <i>n</i>. The full multi-leg eigenstructure is Frobenius compatible with the prescribed arithmetic eigenvalue, and the geometric sheaf has parabolic nilpotent singular support.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $n\geq2$, let $\mathbb F_q$ have characteristic $p>n$, let $\ell\ne p$, and put $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$. |
| [stmt-def-eigenstructure](#stmt-def-eigenstructure) | definition | `def:eigenstructure` | 2 | For a $\check G$-local system $\vartheta$ on $U$, a coherent Hecke eigenstructure on $F$ consists of isomorphisms |
| [stmt-lem-satake-transport](#stmt-lem-satake-transport) | lemma | `lem:satake-transport` | 3 | Semilinear isomorphisms of pointed curves identify the relative Satake and Hecke systems, compatibly with all the operations in Definition 2. |
| [stmt-lem-normalized-weil-satake](#stmt-lem-normalized-weil-satake) | lemma | `lem:normalized-weil-satake` | 4 | Over a finite field, the split Weil structures on [eq:satake-normalization] give a symmetric tensor Satake system, with ordinary coefficient multiplicity spaces carrying trivial action. |
| [stmt-prop-equivariant-specialization](#stmt-prop-equivariant-specialization) | proposition | `prop:equivariant-specialization` | 5 | In this setup, geometric nearby cycles preserve local bounded constructibility and are perverse exact. |
| [stmt-lem-lie-hypotheses](#stmt-lem-lie-hypotheses) | lemma | `lem:lie-hypotheses` | 6 | For $G=\mathrm{SL}_n$ over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero or of characteristic $p>n$, the hypotheses H1–H4 of hold.* |
| [stmt-prop-equivariant-unramified](#stmt-prop-equivariant-unramified) | proposition | `prop:equivariant-unramified` | 7 | Let $C$ be a smooth projective connected curve of genus at least two over an algebraically closed field $K$ of characteristic zero. |
| [stmt-lem-spectral-action-transport](#stmt-lem-spectral-action-transport) | lemma | `lem:spectral-action-transport` | 8 | Semilinear transport by $f$ identifies the automorphic spectral action with the action for the transported curve and its restricted stack of local systems. |
